I just sort of drift without noticing.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself interrupting others when they’re talking, or blurting out answers before someone finishes asking a question?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do that sometimes. Then afterward I’m like, “Why did I cut in?” It’s usually because I think I know the fix and I want to help, but yeah, I can see how it comes off as interrupting.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. (Note: In adolescents or adults, may be limited to feeling restless.)\n  manifestation: Sitting still through a training video or a slow appointment makes me feel kind of boxed in, like I need to stand up, stretch, or do something with my hands.\n- key: S013\n  name: Quiet_Activity_Difficulty\n  description: Often unable to play or engage in leisure activities quietly.\n  manifestation: Quiet stuff that’s supposed to relax me, like reading at home or sitting through a movie with my wife, doesn’t last long before I’m shifting around, commenting, or getting up for something.\n- key: S014\n  name: Constant_Activity\n  description: Is often 'on the go', acting as if 'driven by a motor'.\n  manifestation: What brings me in is that I feel like I’m always moving from one thing to the next, like I can’t just idle, and the new work setup has made that a lot harder to hide.\n- key: S017\n  name: Waiting_Turn_Difficulty\n  description: Often has difficulty waiting his or her turn.\n  manifestation: Waiting for my turn in a team huddle or in the help queue makes me antsy, and I catch myself checking the screen over and over or wanting to jump in before I’m called on.\n- key: S018\n  name: Interrupting_Intruding\n  description: Often interrupts or intrudes on others (e.g., butts into conversations, games, or activities).\n  manifestation: I don’t mean to be rude, but if I hear coworkers working through a customer problem, I’ll jump in with an answer before they’ve finished or before anyone asked me.\n- key: additional_requirement_1\n  name: Additional Requirement 1\n  description: Onset prior to age 12 years\n  manifestation: Looking back, this was around long before this job change; Not every conversation, but especially if it’s a longer coaching meeting at work, I’ll start off listening fine and then a few minutes later I realize I’ve been looking at the person, but I didn’t really take in the last part.\n\nSame with reading a new procedure. My eyes keep going down the page like I’m reading it, and then I stop and think, “Wait, what did that say?” It’s annoying because I’m trying to pay attention, it’s not like I don’t care. I just sort of drift without noticing.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself interrupting others when they’re talking, or blurting out answers before someone finishes asking a question?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do that sometimes. I don’t mean it like I’m trying to take over or be rude, but at work, if I hear a coworker talking through a customer problem, I’ll jump in with the answer before they’re even done explaining it.\n\nSometimes nobody even asked me, I just hear enough of it and my mouth kind of goes before I think. Then afterward I’m like, “Why did I cut in?” It’s usually because I think I know the fix and I want to help, but yeah, I can see how it comes off as interrupting.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down, hopeless, or losing interest in things you usually enjoy, like hobbies or time with family?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to answer that. I mean, I have days where I’m tired of everything, like anybody, especially after dealing with customers all day—some people can really wear you out, you know? I’ll come home and just want the TV on and nobody asking me a bunch of questions.\n\nBut down or hopeless… I don’t know that I’d call it that. And losing interest, I haven’t really noticed. I still like doing the things I like when I can get to them. I still like doing the things I like when I can get to them. I don’t know… I wouldn’t say I walk around thinking something bad is about to happen all the time. I mean, everybody worries some, right?

But that’s not every day exactly.\n\nIt’s funny, because some days at work it depends who calls in—there’s this one kind of customer who just wants to argue about everything, and by the time you’re done you feel like you’ve run a marathon sitting in a chair. And with the new setup at work, it’s been harder to cover that up. I do customer service, and before I could kind of move around or shift tasks, but now it’s more obvious, I guess. It’s just been getting to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt this way for a long time, like even as far back as when you were in school, or is this something that’s more recent?\n\nPatient: No, it’s not just recent. I mean, the job change kind of made it harder to hide, but looking back… yeah, it was there when I was a kid too. In grade school, teachers would write things like I was bright but careless, or that I talked out of turn.\n\nMy mother kept some of those old report cards in a box with, you know, birthday cards and school pictures—she was big on saving everything—and I remember seeing comments about needing reminders to stay with my work. I used to think that was just normal kid stuff, but I guess it was a pattern.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that this restlessness or difficulty staying focused is something that happens in other parts of your life, like at home or when you're with friends, or is it mostly at work?\n\nPatient: No, it’s definitely not just work. At home it shows up too, mostly with quiet things that are supposed to be relaxing. Like I’ll try to read, and after a few minutes I’m shifting in the chair or getting up to check something.\n\nOr my wife and I will sit down for a movie, and I mean, I want to watch it, but I’ll start making comments, pausing it, getting up for a drink or whatever. She’ll say, “Can we just watch the movie?” So yeah, it’s in other places too, not only at work.\n\nDoctor: When you feel restless or have trouble staying focused, do you also find yourself moving around a lot, fidgeting, or feeling like you can't stay still?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. Even when I’m trying to look like I’m sitting still, my foot’ll be bouncing under the desk, or I’ll be clicking a pen and not even realize I’m doing it. Meetings are probably the worst for that.\n\nFunny thing is, I’ll think I’m being pretty calm, and then someone will look over, or I’ll notice the noise myself and stop. But then a minute later I’m doing it again.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself losing track of what people are saying during conversations, or do you feel like your mind wanders a lot even when you're trying to pay attention?\n\nPatient: Yeah, that happens. Not every conversation, but especially if it’s a longer coaching meeting at work, I’ll start off listening fine and then a few minutes later I realize I’ve been looking at the person, but I didn’t really take in the last part.\n\nSame with reading a new procedure. My eyes keep going down the page like I’m reading it, and then I stop and think, “Wait, what did that say?” It’s annoying because I’m trying to pay attention, it’s not like I don’t care. I just sort of drift without noticing.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself interrupting others when they’re talking, or blurting out answers before someone finishes asking a question?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do that sometimes. I don’t mean it like I’m trying to take over or be rude, but at work, if I hear a coworker talking through a customer problem, I’ll jump in with the answer before they’re even done explaining it.\n\nSometimes nobody even asked me, I just hear enough of it and my mouth kind of goes before I think. Then afterward I’m like, “Why did I cut in?” It’s usually because I think I know the fix and I want to help, but yeah, I can see how it comes off as interrupting.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down, hopeless, or losing interest in things you usually enjoy, like hobbies or time with family?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to answer that. I mean, I have days where I’m tired of everything, like anybody, especially after dealing with customers all day—some people can really wear you out, you know? I’ll come home and just want the TV on and nobody asking me a bunch of questions.\n\nBut down or hopeless… I don’t know that I’d call it that. And losing interest, I haven’t really noticed. At least I don’t think so.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel worried or on edge, like something bad might happen, even when things seem okay on the surface?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I wouldn’t say I walk around thinking something bad is about to happen all the time. I mean, everybody worries some, right?\n\nLike if the car makes a weird noise, I’ll think, “Great, what’s that going to cost?” and then I’ll remember this old truck I had years ago that always had something wrong with it—spent half my paycheck keeping that thing running. At least I haven’t really noticed that.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you lose interest or pleasure in activities most of the time, even in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not most of the time. I still enjoy stuff when I actually sit down and do it. But that’s different than not enjoying it, I think. I mean, unless I’m misunderstanding what you mean by “pleasure.”\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced times when you felt unusually high, overly confident, or so energetic that you barely needed sleep?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not like that. I’m not sure I’d say “often.” I mean, I get tired like anybody does, especially after work, but if I haven’t been doing much… I don’t really notice being wiped out for no reason.

It’s just been getting to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt this way for a long time, like even as far back as when you were in school, or is this something that’s more recent?\n\nPatient: No, it’s not just recent. I mean, the job change kind of made it harder to hide, but looking back… yeah, it was there when I was a kid too. In grade school, teachers would write things like I was bright but careless, or that I talked out of turn.\n\nMy mother kept some of those old report cards in a box with, you know, birthday cards and school pictures—she was big on saving everything—and I remember seeing comments about needing reminders to stay with my work. I used to think that was just normal kid stuff, but I guess it was a pattern.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that this restlessness or difficulty staying focused is something that happens in other parts of your life, like at home or when you're with friends, or is it mostly at work?\n\nPatient: No, it’s definitely not just work. At home it shows up too, mostly with quiet things that are supposed to be relaxing. Like I’ll try to read, and after a few minutes I’m shifting in the chair or getting up to check something.\n\nOr my wife and I will sit down for a movie, and I mean, I want to watch it, but I’ll start making comments, pausing it, getting up for a drink or whatever. She’ll say, “Can we just watch the movie?” So yeah, it’s in other places too, not only at work.\n\nDoctor: When you feel restless or have trouble staying focused, do you also find yourself moving around a lot, fidgeting, or feeling like you can't stay still?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. Even when I’m trying to look like I’m sitting still, my foot’ll be bouncing under the desk, or I’ll be clicking a pen and not even realize I’m doing it. Meetings are probably the worst for that.\n\nFunny thing is, I’ll think I’m being pretty calm, and then someone will look over, or I’ll notice the noise myself and stop. But then a minute later I’m doing it again.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself losing track of what people are saying during conversations, or do you feel like your mind wanders a lot even when you're trying to pay attention?\n\nPatient: Yeah, that happens. Not every conversation, but especially if it’s a longer coaching meeting at work, I’ll start off listening fine and then a few minutes later I realize I’ve been looking at the person, but I didn’t really take in the last part.\n\nSame with reading a new procedure. My eyes keep going down the page like I’m reading it, and then I stop and think, “Wait, what did that say?” It’s annoying because I’m trying to pay attention, it’s not like I don’t care. I just sort of drift without noticing.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself interrupting others when they’re talking, or blurting out answers before someone finishes asking a question?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do that sometimes. I don’t mean it like I’m trying to take over or be rude, but at work, if I hear a coworker talking through a customer problem, I’ll jump in with the answer before they’re even done explaining it.\n\nSometimes nobody even asked me, I just hear enough of it and my mouth kind of goes before I think. Then afterward I’m like, “Why did I cut in?” It’s usually because I think I know the fix and I want to help, but yeah, I can see how it comes off as interrupting.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down, hopeless, or losing interest in things you usually enjoy, like hobbies or time with family?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to answer that. I’ll come home and just want the TV on and nobody asking me a bunch of questions.\n\nBut down or hopeless… I don’t know that I’d call it that. And losing interest, I haven’t really noticed. I still like doing the things I like when I can get to them. I guess I’d have to think about it more.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you feel down or lose interest in things more often than not, like on most days?\n\nPatient: Most days? I don’t think so… not that I’ve really noticed. I mean, I can get worn out, like after a long shift, and then I’m kind of flat for the evening. But that’s not every day exactly.\n\nIt’s funny, because some days at work it depends who calls in—there’s this one kind of customer who just wants to argue about everything, and by the time you’re done you feel like you’ve run a marathon sitting in a chair. She’ll say, “Can we just watch the movie?” So yeah, it’s in other places too, not only at work.\n\nDoctor: When you feel restless or have trouble staying focused, do you also find yourself moving around a lot, fidgeting, or feeling like you can't stay still?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. Even when I’m trying to look like I’m sitting still, my foot’ll be bouncing under the desk, or I’ll be clicking a pen and not even realize I’m doing it. Meetings are probably the worst for that.\n\nFunny thing is, I’ll think I’m being pretty calm, and then someone will look over, or I’ll notice the noise myself and stop. But then a minute later I’m doing it again.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself losing track of what people are saying during conversations, or do you feel like your mind wanders a lot even when you're trying to pay attention?\n\nPatient: Yeah, that happens. Not every conversation, but especially if it’s a longer coaching meeting at work, I’ll start off listening fine and then a few minutes later I realize I’ve been looking at the person, but I didn’t really take in the last part.\n\nSame with reading a new procedure. My eyes keep going down the page like I’m reading it, and then I stop and think, “Wait, what did that say?” It’s annoying because I’m trying to pay attention, it’s not like I don’t care. I just sort of drift without noticing.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself interrupting others when they’re talking, or blurting out answers before someone finishes asking a question?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do that sometimes. I don’t mean it like I’m trying to take over or be rude, but at work, if I hear a coworker talking through a customer problem, I’ll jump in with the answer before they’re even done explaining it.\n\nSometimes nobody even asked me, I just hear enough of it and my mouth kind of goes before I think. Then afterward I’m like, “Why did I cut in?” It’s usually because I think I know the fix and I want to help, but yeah, I can see how it comes off as interrupting.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down, hopeless, or losing interest in things you usually enjoy, like hobbies or time with family?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to answer that. I mean, I have days where I’m tired of everything, like anybody, especially after dealing with customers all day—some people can really wear you out, you know? I’ll come home and just want the TV on and nobody asking me a bunch of questions.\n\nBut down or hopeless… I don’t know that I’d call it that. And losing interest, I haven’t really noticed. I still like doing the things I like when I can get to them. I guess I’d have to think about it more.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you feel down or lose interest in things more often than not, like on most days?\n\nPatient: Most days? I don’t think so… not that I’ve really noticed. I mean, I can get worn out, like after a long shift, and then I’m kind of flat for the evening. But that’s not every day exactly.\n\nIt’s funny, because some days at work it depends who calls in—there’s this one kind of customer who just wants to argue about everything, and by the time you’re done you feel like you’ve run a marathon sitting in a chair. So then I might not feel like doing much when I get home. But I wouldn’t say I’m down most days, or that I’ve lost interest in everything. At least I don’t think so.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel worried or on edge, like something bad might happen, even when things seem okay on the surface?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I wouldn’t say I walk around thinking something bad is about to happen all the time. I mean, everybody worries some, right?\n\nLike if the car makes a weird noise, I’ll think, “Great, what’s that going to cost?” and then I’ll remember this old truck I had years ago that always had something wrong with it—spent half my paycheck keeping that thing running. But that’s more just normal life stuff, I think.\n\nOn edge… maybe sometimes if things are hectic, but not like for no reason when everything’s fine. At least I haven’t really noticed that.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you lose interest or pleasure in activities most of the time, even in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not most of the time. I still enjoy stuff when I actually sit down and do it. Like, if there’s a game on or I’m messing around with something in the garage, I can get into it.\n\nIt’s more like sometimes I get home and I’m just done for the day, and then I don’t feel like starting anything. But that’s different than not enjoying it, I think. I mean, unless I’m misunderstanding what you mean by “pleasure.”\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced times when you felt unusually high, overly confident, or so energetic that you barely needed sleep?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not like that. I’ve had nights where I stayed up too late, but that’s usually because I got caught up watching something or I was fiddling with some thing around the house and lost track of time.\n\nLike last year I was trying to fix this cabinet hinge—simple thing, right?—and then I ended up reorganizing half the drawer because I found old batteries and screws everywhere. But I wouldn’t say I felt unusually high or like I didn’t need sleep. Usually the next day I’m tired like anybody else.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel tired or low on energy, even when you haven’t been doing much physically or mentally?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say “often.” I mean, I get tired like anybody does, especially after work, but if I haven’t been doing much… I don’t really notice being wiped out for no reason.\n\nSometimes I’ll sit down and then, you know, once you sit down it’s hard to get back up. My wife used to say that about the recliner—once I’m in that thing, forget it. But that’s more just… I don’t know, getting comfortable, not like I’m drained all the time.\n\nSo I guess no, not really, unless I’m misunderstanding what you mean by low energy.\n\nDoctor: Do your difficulties with staying focused or feeling restless cause problems in your work, relationships, or other important areas of your life?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I wouldn’t say it ruins everything, but it’s started to show up at work more than I’d like.\n\nLike in my reviews, my supervisors have mentioned unfinished tickets, or little mistakes that end up slowing the team down. And I’m trying, you know? I’m not just blowing it off. I usually do okay with the customers themselves—I get along with people pretty well—but then there’ll be some detail I missed or something I didn’t finish before moving on.\n\nIt’s frustrating, because it’s not like I don’t care. I’ll mean to circle back, and then something else comes up, and… yeah, it causes some problems.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Yeah, work is probably the clearest one. Like I said, it’s not that I’m fighting with people or anything—customers usually like me, I think, and I try to be polite and helpful.

My wife used to say that about the recliner—once I’m in that thing, forget it. But that’s more just… I don’t know, getting comfortable, not like I’m drained all the time.\n\nSo I guess no, not really, unless I’m misunderstanding what you mean by low energy.\n\nDoctor: Do your difficulties with staying focused or feeling restless cause problems in your work, relationships, or other important areas of your life?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I wouldn’t say it ruins everything, but it’s started to show up at work more than I’d like.\n\nLike in my reviews, my supervisors have mentioned unfinished tickets, or little mistakes that end up slowing the team down. And I’m trying, you know? I’m not just blowing it off. I usually do okay with the customers themselves—I get along with people pretty well—but then there’ll be some detail I missed or something I didn’t finish before moving on.\n\nIt’s frustrating, because it’s not like I don’t care. I’ll mean to circle back, and then something else comes up, and… yeah, it causes some problems.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of specific times when your difficulty staying focused or feeling restless led to problems in your relationships, at work, or in other important parts of your life?\n\nPatient: Yeah, work is probably the clearest one. Like I said, it’s not that I’m fighting with people or anything—customers usually like me, I think, and I try to be polite and helpful.\n\nBut in my reviews, my supervisors have brought up that I’ll have tickets that aren’t finished, or I’ll miss some little thing that turns into a bigger slowdown for everybody. And that’s embarrassing, because I really am trying. And with the new setup at work, it’s been harder to cover that up. I do customer service, and before I could kind of move around or shift tasks, but now it’s more obvious, I guess. It’s just been getting to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt this way for a long time, like even as far back as when you were in school, or is this something that’s more recent?\n\nPatient: No, it’s not just recent. I mean, the job change kind of made it harder to hide, but looking back… yeah, it was there when I was a kid too. In grade school, teachers would write things like I was bright but careless, or that I talked out of turn.\n\nMy mother kept some of those old report cards in a box with, you know, birthday cards and school pictures—she was big on saving everything—and I remember seeing comments about needing reminders to stay with my work. I used to think that was just normal kid stuff, but I guess it was a pattern.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that this restlessness or difficulty staying focused is something that happens in other parts of your life, like at home or when you're with friends, or is it mostly at work?\n\nPatient: No, it’s definitely not just work. At home it shows up too, mostly with quiet things that are supposed to be relaxing. Like I’ll try to read, and after a few minutes I’m shifting in the chair or getting up to check something.\n\nOr my wife and I will sit down for a movie, and I mean, I want to watch it, but I’ll start making comments, pausing it, getting up for a drink or whatever. She’ll say, “Can we just watch the movie?” So yeah, it’s in other places too, not only at work.\n\nDoctor: When you feel restless or have trouble staying focused, do you also find yourself moving around a lot, fidgeting, or feeling like you can't stay still?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. Even when I’m trying to look like I’m sitting still, my foot’ll be bouncing under the desk, or I’ll be clicking a pen and not even realize I’m doing it. Meetings are probably the worst for that.\n\nFunny thing is, I’ll think I’m being pretty calm, and then someone will look over, or I’ll notice the noise myself and stop. But then a minute later I’m doing it again.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself losing track of what people are saying during conversations, or do you feel like your mind wanders a lot even when you're trying to pay attention?\n\nPatient: Yeah, that happens. Not every conversation, but especially if it’s a longer coaching meeting at work, I’ll start off listening fine and then a few minutes later I realize I’ve been looking at the person, but I didn’t really take in the last part.\n\nSame with reading a new procedure. My eyes keep going down the page like I’m reading it, and then I stop and think, “Wait, what did that say?” It’s annoying because I’m trying to pay attention, it’s not like I don’t care. I just sort of drift without noticing.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself interrupting others when they’re talking, or blurting out answers before someone finishes asking a question?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do that sometimes. I don’t mean it like I’m trying to take over or be rude, but at work, if I hear a coworker talking through a customer problem, I’ll jump in with the answer before they’re even done explaining it.\n\nSometimes nobody even asked me, I just hear enough of it and my mouth kind of goes before I think. Then afterward I’m like, “Why did I cut in?” It’s usually because I think I know the fix and I want to help, but yeah, I can see how it comes off as interrupting.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down, hopeless, or losing interest in things you usually enjoy, like hobbies or time with family?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to answer that. I mean, I have days where I’m tired of everything, like anybody, especially after dealing with customers all day—some people can really wear you out, you know? I’ll come home and just want the TV on and nobody asking me a bunch of questions.\n\nBut down or hopeless… I don’t know that I’d call it that. And losing interest, I haven’t really noticed. I still like doing the things I like when I can get to them. I guess I’d have to think about it more.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you feel down or lose interest in things more often than not, like on most days?\n\nPatient: Most days? I don’t think so… not that I’ve really noticed. I mean, I can get worn out, like after a long shift, and then I’m kind of flat for the evening. But that’s not every day exactly.\n\nIt’s funny, because some days at work it depends who calls in—there’s this one kind of customer who just wants to argue about everything, and by the time you’re done you feel like you’ve run a marathon sitting in a chair. So then I might not feel like doing much when I get home. But I wouldn’t say I’m down most days, or that I’ve lost interest in everything. At least I don’t think so.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel worried or on edge, like something bad might happen, even when things seem okay on the surface?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I wouldn’t say I walk around thinking something bad is about to happen all the time. I mean, everybody worries some, right?\n\nLike if the car makes a weird noise, I’ll think, “Great, what’s that going to cost?” and then I’ll remember this old truck I had years ago that always had something wrong with it—spent half my paycheck keeping that thing running. But that’s more just normal life stuff, I think.\n\nOn edge… maybe sometimes if things are hectic, but not like for no reason when everything’s fine. At least I haven’t really noticed that.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you lose interest or pleasure in activities most of the time, even in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not most of the time. I still enjoy stuff when I actually sit down and do it. Like, if there’s a game on or I’m messing around with something in the garage, I can get into it.\n\nIt’s more like sometimes I get home and I’m just done for the day, and then I don’t feel like starting anything. But that’s different than not enjoying it, I think. I mean, unless I’m misunderstanding what you mean by “pleasure.”\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced times when you felt unusually high, overly confident, or so energetic that you barely needed sleep?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not like that. I’ve had nights where I stayed up too late, but that’s usually because I got caught up watching something or I was fiddling with some thing around the house and lost track of time.\n\nLike last year I was trying to fix this cabinet hinge—simple thing, right?—and then I ended up reorganizing half the drawer because I found old batteries and screws everywhere. But I wouldn’t say I felt unusually high or like I didn’t need sleep. Usually the next day I’m tired like anybody else.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel tired or low on energy, even when you haven’t been doing much physically or mentally?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say “often.” I mean, I get tired like anybody does, especially after work, but if I haven’t been doing much… I don’t really notice being wiped out for no reason.\n\nSometimes I’ll sit down and then, you know, once you sit down it’s hard to get back up. My wife used to say that about the recliner—once I’m in that thing, forget it. But that’s more just… I don’t know, getting comfortable, not like I’m drained all the time.\n\nSo I guess no, not really, unless I’m misunderstanding what you mean by low energy.\n\nDoctor: Do your difficulties with staying focused or feeling restless cause problems in your work, relationships, or other important areas of your life?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I wouldn’t say it ruins everything, but it’s started to show up at work more than I’d like.\n\nLike in my reviews, my supervisors have mentioned unfinished tickets, or little mistakes that end up slowing the team down. And I’m trying, you know? I’m not just blowing it off. I usually do okay with the customers themselves—I get along with people pretty well—but then there’ll be some detail I missed or something I didn’t finish before moving on.\n\nIt’s frustrating, because it’s not like I don’t care. I’ll mean to circle back, and then something else comes up, and… yeah, it causes some problems.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of specific times when your difficulty staying focused or feeling restless led to problems in your relationships, at work, or in other important parts of your life?\n\nPatient: Yeah, work is probably the clearest one. Like I said, it’s not that I’m fighting with people or anything—customers usually like me, I think, and I try to be polite and helpful.\n\nBut in my reviews, my supervisors have brought up that I’ll have tickets that aren’t finished, or I’ll miss some little thing that turns into a bigger slowdown for everybody. And that’s embarrassing, because I really am trying. It’s not like I sit there thinking, “Oh, I don’t care.” I’ll start one thing, then something else comes in, and then later someone’s asking why the first thing wasn’t wrapped up.\n\nIt reminds me of when you open one drawer at home and suddenly you’re fixing three other things—but at work, obviously, people notice. So yeah, that’s where it’s caused problems the most.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you lose interest or pleasure in activities most of the time, even in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not most of the time. I mean, I still enjoy things when I actually get around to doing them.\n\nLike, if there’s a game on or I’m messing around in the garage, I can still get into it. Sometimes I’ll put something off and then end up watching the same show reruns, but that’s not really because I don’t like things anymore—it’s more just… I don’t know, getting started is its own whole thing sometimes.\n\nSo no, I wouldn’t say I’ve lost interest or pleasure in stuff across the board.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
